    "id": "Vampir Croglin Grange adalah legenda vampir dari Cumberland, Inggris, yang pertama kali dicatat Augustus Hare pada 1890-an dan keasliannya diragukan para peneliti.",
    "en": "The Vampire of Croglin Grange is a vampire legend from Cumberland, England, first recorded by Augustus Hare in the 1890s, whose authenticity researchers doubt.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vampir Croglin Grange adalah legenda vampir yang berlatar di Cumberland, Inggris. Kisah ini pertama kali muncul dalam Story of My Life karya Augustus Hare yang ditulis pada 1890-an. Menurut Hare, peristiwanya terjadi antara 1875 dan 1876, ketika Amelia, Edward, dan Michael Cranswell menyewa sebuah rumah tua.",
      "en": "The Vampire of Croglin Grange is a vampire legend set in Cumberland, England. The story first appeared in Augustus Hare's Story of My Life, written in the 1890s. According to Hare, the events took place between 1875 and 1876, when Amelia, Edward and Michael Cranswell rented an old house.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "croglin-grange-c01",
        "croglin-grange-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suatu malam musim panas, makhluk berwajah cokelat dan bermata menyala mencungkil timah jendela kamar Amelia dengan kuku panjang, masuk, dan menggigit lehernya. Ketika makhluk itu kembali, salah satu saudaranya menembak kakinya dan melacaknya ke ruang makam di pemakaman setempat. Di sana mereka menemukan jasad vampir dengan luka baru di kaki di dalam peti, lalu membakarnya.",
      "en": "One summer night a creature with a brown face and flaming eyes picked out the lead of Amelia's window with a long fingernail, got in and bit her throat. When it returned, one of her brothers shot it in the leg and tracked it to a vault in the local cemetery. There they found the vampire's body in a coffin with a fresh leg wound, and burned it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "croglin-grange-c05",
        "croglin-grange-c06",
        "croglin-grange-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Keaslian kisah ini diragukan. Pada 1924 Charles G. Harper tidak menemukan bukti bahwa Croglin Grange pernah ada, hanya dua bangunan serupa. Pada 1929 Montague Summers menunjukkan kemiripannya dengan Varney the Vampire dan menganggapnya cerita rakyat belaka. Satu versinya muncul sebagai \"The Window\" dalam More Scary Stories to Tell in the Dark (1984).",
      "en": "The story's authenticity is doubted. In 1924 Charles G. Harper found no evidence that Croglin Grange ever existed, only two similar buildings. In 1929 Montague Summers pointed out its similarity to Varney the Vampire and dismissed it as folklore. A version appears as \"The Window\" in More Scary Stories to Tell in the Dark (1984).",

      "id": "croglin-grange-c01",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "The Vampire of Croglin Grange is a vampire legend that took place in Cumberland, England. The story first appeared in Story of My Life by Augustus Hare, written in the 1890s.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vampir Croglin Grange adalah legenda vampir yang berlatar di Cumberland, Inggris; kisahnya pertama kali muncul dalam Story of My Life karya Augustus Hare yang ditulis pada 1890-an.",
        "en": "The Vampire of Croglin Grange is a vampire legend set in Cumberland, England; the story first appeared in Augustus Hare's Story of My Life, written in the 1890s."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c02",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "In 1929, Montague Summers republished the story along with the first chapter of Varney the Vampire. He pointed out that the two stories were very similar and should be dismissed as folklore.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1929 Montague Summers menerbitkan ulang kisah itu bersama bab pertama Varney the Vampire, dan menunjukkan bahwa kedua cerita sangat mirip sehingga sebaiknya dianggap cerita rakyat belaka.",
        "en": "In 1929 Montague Summers republished the story with the first chapter of Varney the Vampire, pointing out that the two stories were very similar and should be dismissed as folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c03",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "Charles G. Harper challenged the Hare book in 1924 after visiting the area. He found no evidence that Croglin Grange ever even existed, but did however find two similar buildings called Croglin High Hall and Croglin Low Hall.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Charles G. Harper menggugat buku Hare pada 1924 setelah mengunjungi daerah itu; ia tidak menemukan bukti bahwa Croglin Grange pernah ada, tetapi menemukan dua bangunan serupa bernama Croglin High Hall dan Croglin Low Hall.",
        "en": "Charles G. Harper challenged Hare's book in 1924 after visiting the area; he found no evidence that Croglin Grange ever existed but found two similar buildings called Croglin High Hall and Croglin Low Hall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c04",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "In the tale written by Augustus Hare, several episodes took place between 1875 and 1876. An old house had been rented out to a woman and two brothers, Amelia, Edward, and Michael Cranswell.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Augustus Hare, peristiwanya terjadi antara 1875 dan 1876, ketika sebuah rumah tua disewakan kepada seorang perempuan dan dua saudara laki-lakinya, Amelia, Edward, dan Michael Cranswell.",
        "en": "In Augustus Hare's tale the episodes took place between 1875 and 1876, when an old house was rented to a woman and her two brothers, Amelia, Edward and Michael Cranswell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c05",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "During one summer, Amelia was trying to sleep when a strange creature appeared at her window and began picking out the lead surrounding one of the window panes with a long fingernail",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada suatu musim panas, saat Amelia hendak tidur, makhluk aneh muncul di jendelanya dan mulai mencungkil timah di sekeliling salah satu kaca jendela dengan kuku panjang.",
        "en": "One summer, as Amelia was trying to sleep, a strange creature appeared at her window and began picking out the lead around one of the panes with a long fingernail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c06",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "It was described as having a brown face and flaming eyes. The vampire bit her in the throat.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk itu digambarkan berwajah cokelat dan bermata menyala; vampir itu menggigit lehernya.",
        "en": "It was described as having a brown face and flaming eyes; the vampire bit her in the throat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c07",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "The brother shot it in the leg and was able to track it down to a vault in the local cemetery. They waited until the next day to enter the vault, where they found the body of the vampire, with a fresh wound to the leg, resting inside a coffin. They then burned it.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saudara Amelia menembak kaki makhluk itu dan melacaknya ke sebuah ruang makam di pemakaman setempat; keesokan harinya mereka menemukan jasad vampir dengan luka baru di kaki terbaring dalam peti, lalu membakarnya.",
        "en": "Amelia's brother shot it in the leg and tracked it to a vault in the local cemetery; the next day they found the vampire's body, with a fresh leg wound, lying in a coffin, and burned it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "croglin-grange-c08",
      "source_id": "croglin-grange-s1",
      "quote": "A version of the story appears under the title \"The Window\" in Alvin Schwartz and Stephen Gammell's More Scary Stories to Tell in the Dark (1984).",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu versi kisah ini muncul dengan judul \"The Window\" dalam More Scary Stories to Tell in the Dark (1984) karya Alvin Schwartz dan Stephen Gammell.",
        "en": "A version of the story appears as \"The Window\" in Alvin Schwartz and Stephen Gammell's More Scary Stories to Tell in the Dark (1984)."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Deattán adalah roh dalam cerita rakyat Sami yang berwujud burung atau hewan dan duduk di dada orang tidur, menyebabkan mimpi buruk.",
    "en": "Deattán is a spirit of Sami lore in the form of a bird or animal that sits on a sleeper's chest and causes nightmares.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "deattan-c01",
      "deattan-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Sami, Deattán atau Painajainen adalah roh yang muncul dalam wujud burung atau hewan lain. Pada malam hari ia duduk di dada orang yang sedang tidur. Ia menyebabkan mimpi buruk, kegelisahan, dan tidur yang buruk, bahkan bisa merampas tidur sama sekali.",
      "en": "In Sami lore Deattán or Painajainen is a spirit appearing in the form of a bird or other animal. At night it sits on the chest of a sleeping person. It causes nightmares, anxiety and poor sleep, and can even take sleep away entirely.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "deattan-c01",
        "deattan-c02"
      ]
    }
  ],

Ia membawa botol berisi darah saat mencari sarang vampir dan memakai ikon Yesus, Perawan Maria, orang suci, atau relikui sebagai pelindung; bila ikon bergetar, vampir sudah dekat.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Floçka atau Gërzheta adalah makhluk mirip nimfa berambut sangat panjang dari paganisme Albania, penghuni dan pelindung danau serta sungai di pegunungan.",
    "en": "The Floçka or Gërzheta is a nymph-like, very long-haired creature of Albanian paganism, dwelling in and protecting mountain lakes and rivers.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "flocka-c01",
      "flocka-c02",
      "flocka-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Floçka, juga disebut Gërzheta, adalah makhluk mirip nimfa dari paganisme Albania. Mereka tinggal di dekat perairan seperti danau dan pulau di bagian tersembunyi pegunungan, dikatakan amat cantik, dan dipercaya melindungi danau dan sungai. Namanya berasal dari kata Albania untuk rambut, \"flokë\", karena rambut mereka sangat panjang; penyair Gjergj Fishta menyebut mereka \"Para Jelita Laut\".",
      "en": "The Floçka, also called Gërzheta, is a nymph-like creature of Albanian paganism. They live near waters such as lakes and islands in hidden parts of the mountains, are said to be immensely beautiful, and are believed to protect lakes and rivers. The name comes from the Albanian word for hair, \"flokë\", because of their very long hair; the poet Gjergj Fishta calls them \"The Beauties of the Sea\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "flocka-c01",
        "flocka-c02",
        "flocka-c03",
        "flocka-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Berbeda dari makhluk mitologis Albania lain, Floçka tidak berbahasa Albania, meskipun bisa diajari. Menurut sejarawan Daniel Gjeçaj, kepercayaan akan Floçka pertama kali dianut leluhur suku Kelmendi di Shkodër.",
      "en": "Unlike other Albanian mythological creatures, the Floçka do not speak Albanian, though they can be taught. According to the historian Daniel Gjeçaj, belief in the Floçka was first held by the ancestors of the Kelmendi tribe of Shkodër.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "flocka-c04",
        "flocka-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam \"Legenda Gërzheta\", seorang gembala bungsu melihat tiga Floçka berenang di danau. Bersama saudara-saudaranya ia menjebak salah satunya dengan umpan, lalu menikahinya; ia melahirkan seorang putra tetapi tak pernah berbicara. Setelah lima tahun, sang suami mengancam putra mereka dengan pisau agar istrinya bicara, dan karena sumpahnya dilanggar, Floçka itu mengambil kembali benda-benda umpan dan melarikan diri.",
      "en": "In the \"Legend of the Gërzheta\", a youngest shepherd sees three Floçka swimming in a lake. With his brothers he traps one with bait and marries her; she bears a son but never speaks. After five years the husband threatens their son with a knife to make her speak, and because he broke his oath, the Floçka takes back the bait items and runs away.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "flocka-c07",
        "flocka-c08",
        "flocka-c09",
        "flocka-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The Floçka also known as the \"Gërzheta\" is a nymph-like creature from Albanian paganism. They are described as inhabiting the lands near bodies of water such as lakes and islands in hidden parts of the mountains. The Floçka are said to have immense beauty.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Floçka, juga dikenal sebagai Gërzheta, adalah makhluk mirip nimfa dari paganisme Albania yang tinggal di dekat perairan seperti danau dan pulau di bagian tersembunyi pegunungan, dan dikatakan amat cantik.",
        "en": "The Floçka, also known as the Gërzheta, is a nymph-like creature of Albanian paganism, living near waters such as lakes and islands in hidden parts of the mountains and said to be immensely beautiful."

    {
      "id": "flocka-c02",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",

    {
      "id": "flocka-c03",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "Albanian poet Gjergj Fishta also refers to them as \"Të Bukurat e Detit\" (The beauties of the sea).",

      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The Albanian poet Gjergj Fishta also calls them \"Të Bukurat e Detit\" (The Beauties of the Sea)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c04",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "They do not speak the Albanian language as opposed to other mythological creatures from Albanian paganism, however it can be taught to them.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dengan makhluk mitologis Albania lainnya, mereka tidak berbahasa Albania, tetapi bahasa itu bisa diajarkan kepada mereka.",
        "en": "Unlike other mythological creatures of Albanian paganism, they do not speak Albanian, though it can be taught to them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c05",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "The Floçka's are also believed to serve as protectors of lakes and rivers.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Floçka juga dipercaya menjadi pelindung danau dan sungai.",
        "en": "The Floçka are also believed to protect lakes and rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c06",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "According to Albanian historian Daniel Gjeçaj, the Floçka were firstly believed by the ancestors of the Kelmendi tribe of Shkodër.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sejarawan Albania Daniel Gjeçaj, Floçka pertama kali dipercayai oleh leluhur suku Kelmendi di Shkodër.",
        "en": "According to the Albanian historian Daniel Gjeçaj, the Floçka were first believed in by the ancestors of the Kelmendi tribe of Shkodër."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c07",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "In the legend, a shepherd who is the youngest out of the 4 sons in his family tries to find a wife, as he was the only brother that was not married. While hearding sheep in the mountain, he spots 3 Floçka's swimming in a lake.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam \"Legenda Gërzheta\", seorang gembala, bungsu dari 4 bersaudara dan satu-satunya yang belum menikah, mencari istri; saat menggembala di gunung ia melihat 3 Floçka berenang di danau.",
        "en": "In the \"Legend of the Gërzheta\", a shepherd, the youngest of 4 sons and the only one unmarried, seeks a wife; herding sheep in the mountains he sees 3 Floçka swimming in a lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c08",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "One of the Floçka's left the lake and one by one began grabbing each item before falling into the hole and being captured by the brothers. She would marry the youngest brother and would also give birth to a son, however she would never speak.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu Floçka keluar dari danau, mengambil satu per satu benda yang dipasang sebagai umpan, lalu jatuh ke lubang dan ditangkap para saudara; ia menikah dengan si bungsu dan melahirkan seorang putra, tetapi tidak pernah berbicara.",
        "en": "One of the Floçka left the lake and took the bait items one by one before falling into a pit and being caught by the brothers; she married the youngest and bore a son, but never spoke."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c09",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "After five years of marriage, the husband, wanting to hear his wives voice held a knife at their sons throat threatening to kill him if she didnt speak.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah lima tahun menikah, sang suami yang ingin mendengar suara istrinya menodongkan pisau ke leher putra mereka dan mengancam membunuhnya bila sang istri tidak berbicara.",
        "en": "After five years of marriage the husband, wanting to hear his wife's voice, held a knife to their son's throat, threatening to kill him unless she spoke."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "flocka-c10",
      "source_id": "flocka-s1",
      "quote": "Upon learning their location, she took them and ran away due to the husband breaking his oath.",
      "locator": "Appearance and characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah mengetahui tempat benda-benda umpan itu, ia mengambilnya dan melarikan diri karena sang suami telah melanggar sumpahnya.",
        "en": "On learning where the bait items were, she took them and ran away because her husband had broken his oath."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Roh hutan dalam mitologi Polandia, seperti borowy, boruta, dan leśny duch, bisa tak terlihat atau berwujud hewan, menyesatkan orang di hutan tetapi kadang juga menolong.",
    "en": "The forest spirits of Polish mythology, such as borowy, boruta and leśny duch, could be invisible or take animal form, leading people astray in the forest but sometimes helping them.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c01",
      "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c02",
      "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c03",
      "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Roh hutan dalam mitologi Polandia dikenal dengan banyak nama, antara lain borowiec, borowik, borowy, dan boruta dari kata \"bór\", serta leśnik, leśny duch, dan leśny dziad dari kata \"las\". Menurut berbagai sumber, mereka bisa tak terlihat atau muncul sebagai hewan hutan seperti burung hantu, rusa, beruang, atau babi hutan, maupun hewan ternak seperti kucing, kuda, dan anjing hitam.",
      "en": "Forest spirits in Polish mythology are known by many names, including borowiec, borowik, borowy and boruta from \"bór\", and leśnik, leśny duch and leśny dziad from \"las\". According to various sources they could be invisible or appear as forest animals such as owls, deer, bears or boars, or as domestic animals such as cats, horses and black dogs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c01",
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka menakut-nakuti orang pada malam hari di hutan dan bendungan, menyesatkan mereka, menumpahkan jamur dan buah beri dari keranjang, dan menculik gadis untuk dijadikan istri. Namun mereka juga bisa menuntun orang keluar dari hutan dan memberi hadiah kepada orang yang disukai.",
      "en": "They frightened people at night in forests and on dams, led them astray, spilled mushrooms and berries from their baskets and abducted girls as wives. Yet they could also guide people out of the forest and reward those they liked.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c03",
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Roh-roh ini punya beberapa kemiripan dengan leshy Slavia Timur, roh hutan Slavia yang paling terdokumentasi. Pada bangsa Slavia Barat, peran pelindung hewan hutan dan pemburu kemudian beralih kepada Santo Hubertus, dan peran pelindung dari serigala kepada Santo Nikolaus.",
      "en": "These spirits share some similarities with the East Slavic leshy, the best documented Slavic forest spirit. Among the West Slavs, the role of protector of forest animals and hunters later passed to Saint Hubert, and that of protector against wolves to Saint Nicholas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c05",
        "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9B%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B5_%D0%B4%D1%83%D1%85%D0%B8_%D0%B2_%D0%BF%D0%BE%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B9_%D0%BC%D0%B8%D1%84%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B8",
      "title": "Лесные духи в польской мифологии",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Лесные духи в польской мифологии встречаются под множеством названий: от слова бор — пол. borowiec, borowik, borowy, boruta (сосна по-древнепольски); от слова лес — leśnik, lasowy, leśny, leśny duch, leśny dziad",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh hutan dalam mitologi Polandia punya banyak nama: dari kata \"bór\" (hutan pinus) muncul borowiec, borowik, borowy, boruta; dari kata \"las\" (hutan) muncul leśnik, lasowy, leśny, leśny duch, leśny dziad.",
        "en": "Forest spirits in Polish mythology go by many names: from \"bór\" (pine forest) come borowiec, borowik, borowy, boruta; from \"las\" (forest) come leśnik, lasowy, leśny, leśny duch, leśny dziad."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Согласно разным источникам, они могли быть невидимыми; являться в виде лесного животного — совы, оленя или оленихи, медведя, рыси, кабана, или домашнего животного — барана, кота, коня, чёрного пса",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut berbagai sumber, mereka bisa tak terlihat atau muncul sebagai hewan hutan (burung hantu, rusa jantan atau betina, beruang, lynx, babi hutan) maupun hewan ternak (domba jantan, kucing, kuda, anjing hitam).",
        "en": "According to various sources they could be invisible or appear as forest animals (owl, stag or doe, bear, lynx, boar) or domestic animals (ram, cat, horse, black dog)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Они пугали людей ночью в лесах и на плотинах, заставляли их блуждать, высыпали из корзин набранные грибы и ягоды, похищали девушек, чтобы взять себе в жёны.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menakut-nakuti orang pada malam hari di hutan dan di bendungan, membuat orang tersesat, menumpahkan jamur dan buah beri dari keranjang, dan menculik gadis untuk dijadikan istri.",
        "en": "They frightened people at night in forests and on dams, led them astray, spilled gathered mushrooms and berries from baskets, and abducted girls to take as wives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Но могли и помочь выйти из леса, одарить понравившегося",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun mereka juga bisa membantu orang keluar dari hutan dan memberi hadiah kepada orang yang mereka sukai.",
        "en": "But they could also help people out of the forest and reward someone they liked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Лучше всего из славянских лесных духов описан восточнославянский леший (его также в некоторых регионах называют боровым и боровиком), с которым польские духи имеют некоторое сходство.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di antara roh hutan Slavia, yang paling terdokumentasi adalah leshy Slavia Timur (di beberapa daerah juga disebut borovoi dan borovik), yang punya beberapa kemiripan dengan roh hutan Polandia.",
        "en": "Of the Slavic forest spirits, the best described is the East Slavic leshy (in some regions also called borovoi and borovik), with which the Polish spirits share some similarities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "forest-spirits-in-polish-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "у западных славян функции покровителя лесных зверей и охотников полностью перешли от лесных духов к святому Хуберту, а защитника людей от волков — к святому Николаю",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada bangsa Slavia Barat, peran pelindung hewan hutan dan pemburu sepenuhnya beralih dari roh hutan kepada Santo Hubertus, dan peran pelindung manusia dari serigala kepada Santo Nikolaus.",
        "en": "Among the West Slavs, the role of protector of forest animals and hunters passed entirely from the forest spirits to Saint Hubert, and that of protector against wolves to Saint Nicholas."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gabartai adalah makhluk mitos dalam mitologi Lituania yang dekat dengan kaukai; fungsi dan asal-usul namanya masih tidak jelas.",
    "en": "The gabartai are mythical beings of Lithuanian mythology close to the kaukai; their functions and the origin of their name remain unclear.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gabartai-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Lituania, gabartai atau gabvartai adalah makhluk mitos yang dekat dengan kaukai. Mereka pertama kali disebut pada 1666 dalam buku nyanyian D. Klein, tetapi tanpa keterangan fungsi, dan pada 1673 T. Schultz menyebut Gabvartas setelah kaukas.",
      "en": "In Lithuanian mythology the gabartai or gabvartai are mythical beings close to the kaukai. They were first mentioned in 1666 in D. Klein's hymnal, without their functions, and in 1673 T. Schultz mentions Gabvartas after the kaukas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gabartai-c01",
        "gabartai-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut M. Praetorius, mereka identik dengan kaukai, dewa-dewa bumi pembawa kekayaan. A. J. Greimas mengaitkan mereka dengan gerbang lumbung pengering (jauja), tempat tinggal Gabjaujis. Namun secara umum masalah gabartai tetap tidak jelas, dan etimologinya pun belum terang.",
      "en": "According to M. Praetorius they are identical with the kaukai, earth gods who bring wealth. A. J. Greimas links them with the gates of the drying barn (jauja), home of Gabjaujis. Overall, however, the question of the gabartai remains unclear, as does their etymology.",

        "gabartai-c03",
        "gabartai-c04"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "Gabartai (Gabvartai) lietuvių mitologijoje – mitinės būtybės, artimos kaukams.",
      "locator": "įvadas",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Lituania, gabartai (gabvartai) adalah makhluk mitos yang dekat dengan kaukai (roh pembawa kekayaan).",
        "en": "In Lithuanian mythology the gabartai (gabvartai) are mythical beings close to the kaukai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gabartai-c02",
      "source_id": "gabartai-s1",
      "quote": "Pirmą kartą paminėtos 1666 m. D. Kleino giesmyne, tačiau funkcijos nepaminėtos. 1673 m. T. Šulcas Gabvartą mini po kauko.",
      "locator": "įvadas",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka pertama kali disebut pada 1666 dalam buku nyanyian D. Klein, tanpa keterangan fungsi; pada 1673 T. Schultz menyebut Gabvartas setelah kaukas.",
        "en": "They were first mentioned in 1666 in D. Klein's hymnal, without their functions; in 1673 T. Schultz mentions Gabvartas after the kaukas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gabartai-c03",
      "source_id": "gabartai-s1",
      "quote": "Pagal M. Pretorijų jie yra tapatus kaukams, žemės dievams, kurie neša turtus. Etimologija neaiški.",
      "locator": "įvadas",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut M. Praetorius, mereka identik dengan kaukai, dewa-dewa bumi yang membawa kekayaan; etimologinya tidak jelas.",
        "en": "According to M. Praetorius they are identical with the kaukai, earth gods who bring wealth; the etymology is unclear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gabartai-c04",
      "source_id": "gabartai-s1",
      "quote": "A. J. Greimas juos sieja su jaujos vartais, pagal jį jaujoje gyvena Gabjaujis (Gabjauja), o jaują nuo viso kluono atskiria gabvartai. Tačiau apskritai gabartų klausimas yra neaiškus.",
      "locator": "įvadas",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "A. J. Greimas mengaitkan mereka dengan gerbang lumbung pengering (jauja): menurutnya Gabjaujis (Gabjauja) tinggal di jauja, dan gabvartai memisahkan jauja dari bagian lumbung lainnya; namun secara umum masalah gabartai tetap tidak jelas.",
        "en": "A. J. Greimas links them with the gates of the drying barn (jauja): in his view Gabjaujis (Gabjauja) lives in the jauja, and the gabvartai separate the jauja from the rest of the barn; but overall the question of the gabartai remains unclear."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Hakikat gabartai",
        "en": "Nature of the gabartai"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Identik dengan kaukai, dewa bumi pembawa kekayaan (Praetorius).",
            "en": "Identical with the kaukai, wealth-bringing earth gods (Praetorius)."
          },

          ]
        },
        {

            "en": "Linked with the gates of the drying barn (Greimas)."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "gabartai-c04"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Roh kapal dalam mitologi Liv adalah penjaga dan pemandu kapal yang memperingatkan pelaut akan badai atau karam dengan bunyi ketukan dan rintihan.",
    "en": "The ship spirit of Livonian mythology is the guardian and guide of ships, warning sailors of storms or wrecks by knocking and moaning.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ghost-of-ship-c01",
      "ghost-of-ship-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Liv, roh kapal, hantu kapal, atau roh laut adalah penjaga dan pemandu kapal dan perahu. Ia memperingatkan bencana yang mendekat, seperti badai, kandas, atau karam, dengan bunyi ketukan, kadang dengan rintihan, tangisan, dan teriakan. Kadang ia memberi petunjuk kepada pelaut dalam bahasa manusia dan bersikap keras terhadap yang tidak patuh. Roh ini konon meninggalkan kapal yang tua dan lapuk, dan begitu ia pergi, kapal itu binasa.",
      "en": "In Livonian mythology the ship spirit, ship ghost or sea spirit is the guardian and guide of ships and boats. It warns of approaching disaster, such as storms, stranding or wreck, by knocking and rattling, sometimes by moans, weeping and cries. Sometimes it instructs sailors in human language and is harsh to those who disobey. The spirit is said to leave old, rotting vessels, and once it has gone the ship perishes.",

        "ghost-of-ship-c02",
        "ghost-of-ship-c03",

      "quote": "Kuģa gars (lībiešu: kuo’ig garā), kuģa spoks (kuo’ig spūok) jeb jūras gars (mie’r garā) lībiešu mitoloģijā ir kuģu un laivu sargātājs un vadītājs",
      "locator": "ievads",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Liv, roh kapal (bahasa Liv: kuo’ig garā), hantu kapal (kuo’ig spūok), atau roh laut (mie’r garā) adalah penjaga dan pemandu kapal dan perahu.",
        "en": "In Livonian mythology the ship spirit (Livonian kuo’ig garā), ship ghost (kuo’ig spūok) or sea spirit (mie’r garā) is the guardian and guide of ships and boats."
      }

    {
      "id": "ghost-of-ship-c02",

      "quote": "kas ar klaudzēšanu, klaudzināšanu vai dažkārt pat vaidiem, raudām un kliedzieniem brīdina par tuvojošos nelaimi, piemēram, vētru, kuģa strandēšanu vai bojāeju.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memperingatkan bencana yang mendekat, seperti badai, kapal kandas, atau tenggelam, dengan bunyi ketukan, kadang bahkan dengan rintihan, tangisan, dan teriakan.",
        "en": "It warns of approaching disaster, such as a storm, stranding or wreck, by knocking and rattling, sometimes even by moans, weeping and cries."

      "id": "ghost-of-ship-c03",
      "source_id": "ghost-of-ship-s1",
      "quote": "Dažkārt kuģa gars šādos gadījumos jūrniekiem mēdzot dod norādījumus arī cilvēku valodā, un esot bargs pret tiem, kas viņu neklausa.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang dalam keadaan seperti itu roh kapal memberi petunjuk kepada pelaut dalam bahasa manusia, dan konon keras terhadap mereka yang tidak mematuhinya.",
        "en": "Sometimes in such cases the ship spirit gives sailors instructions in human language, and is said to be harsh to those who do not obey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghost-of-ship-c04",
      "source_id": "ghost-of-ship-s1",
      "quote": "No veciem un satrunējušiem kuģiem un laivām kuģa gars aizejot, un tiklīdz to ir izdarījis kuģis ejot bojā.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh kapal konon meninggalkan kapal dan perahu yang tua dan lapuk, dan begitu ia pergi, kapal itu akan binasa.",
        "en": "The ship spirit is said to leave old, rotting ships and boats, and as soon as it does, the ship perishes."
      }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hejkal adalah demon hutan dalam mitologi Ceko dan Slavia, berwujud lelaki berbulu atau berlumut, yang namanya berasal dari seruannya yang mengerikan.",
    "en": "The hejkal is a forest demon of Czech and Slavic mythology, a fur- or moss-covered man named after its terrifying call.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hejkal-c01",
      "hejkal-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Ceko dan Slavia, hejkal adalah demon hutan yang juga disebut hýkal, hejkadlo, atau manusia liar. Namanya berasal dari seruan mengerikan yang ia keluarkan. Ia berwujud lelaki berbulu atau berlumut dengan seikat pakis di kepala, dan hejkal paling kuat konon ada di wilayah Plzeň.",
      "en": "In Czech and Slavic mythology the hejkal is a forest demon also called hýkal, hejkadlo or wild man. Its name comes from the terrifying call it makes. It looks like a man covered in fur or moss with a bunch of ferns on its head, and the most powerful hejkals were said to live in the Plzeň region.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hejkal-c01",
        "hejkal-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Membalas seruan hejkal sangat berbahaya, karena ia lalu melompat ke punggung orang dan menggiringnya melintasi hutan atau mencabiknya. Hejkal menangkap gadis untuk dijadikan istri, menakut-nakuti pengembara, dan menyesatkan mereka. Orang melindungi diri dengan menukar sepatu, membalik saku, dan membawa roti.",
      "en": "Answering the hejkal's call is very dangerous, since it then leaps on the person's back and drives them through the forest or tears them apart. Hejkals catch girls as wives, frighten travellers and lead them astray. People protected themselves by swapping their shoes, turning out their pockets and carrying bread.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hejkal-c02",
        "hejkal-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Comenius sudah menyebut makhluk ini dan menganggapnya rekaan. Hejkal mirip leshy Slavia Timur serta faun, satir, dan dusii, dan padanan perempuannya adalah hejkalky, divoženky, dan gadis hutan. Ia juga muncul dalam dongeng, misalnya dongeng televisi Cekoslowakia Hejkal tahun 1978.",
      "en": "Comenius already mentioned these beings and considered them invented. The hejkal resembles the East Slavic leshy as well as fauns, satyrs and dusii, and its female counterparts are the hejkalky, divoženky and forest maidens. It also appears in fairy tales, such as the 1978 Czechoslovak television tale Hejkal.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hejkal-c04",
        "hejkal-c05",
        "hejkal-c06",
        "hejkal-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "learning_questions": [

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "hejkal-s1",
      "url": "https://cs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejkal",
      "title": "Hejkal",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (cs)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "cs",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c01",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Hejkal je v českém a slovanském bájesloví lesní démon, nazývaný také hýkal či hejkadlo, nebo divý muž. Jeho jméno je odvozeno od děsivého volání, které vydává.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Ceko dan Slavia, hejkal adalah demon hutan yang juga disebut hýkal, hejkadlo, atau divý muž (manusia liar); namanya berasal dari seruan mengerikan yang ia keluarkan.",
        "en": "In Czech and Slavic mythology the hejkal is a forest demon also called hýkal, hejkadlo or divý muž (wild man); its name comes from the terrifying call it makes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c02",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Podle pověstí je zvláště nebezpečné hejkalovo volání opětovat, ten poté skočí člověku na záda a prožene ho po lese či jej roztrhá. Nejmocnější hejkalové se údajně vyskytovali na Plzeňsku. Mají podobu mužů pokrytých srstí či mechem a na hlavě nosí kytici kapradin.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, membalas seruan hejkal sangat berbahaya: ia lalu melompat ke punggung orang dan menggiringnya berlari melintasi hutan atau mencabiknya; hejkal paling kuat konon ada di wilayah Plzeň, dan mereka berwujud lelaki berbulu atau berlumut dengan seikat pakis di kepala.",
        "en": "According to legend it is especially dangerous to answer the hejkal's call: it then leaps on the person's back and drives them through the forest or tears them apart; the most powerful hejkals were said to live in the Plzeň region, and they look like men covered in fur or moss with a bunch of ferns on their heads."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c03",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Chytají dívky a berou si je za ženy, pokud utečou, roztrhají jejich dítě. Rádi také děsí poutníky a odvádí je z cesty. Chrání před nimi přezutí, obrácení kapes a chléb.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menangkap gadis dan menjadikannya istri, dan jika gadis itu kabur, mereka mencabik anaknya; mereka juga suka menakut-nakuti pengembara dan menyesatkannya; perlindungan terhadap mereka adalah menukar sepatu, membalik saku, dan roti.",
        "en": "They catch girls and take them as wives, and if the girls flee they tear apart their child; they also like to frighten travellers and lead them astray; protection against them is swapping one's shoes, turning out pockets and bread."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c04",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Zmiňuje se o nich již Jan Amos Komenský, který při výkladu slov fauni et satyri „Lesní bohové a lesní muži (chlupáči) vymyšlené věci jsou“.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka sudah disebut oleh Jan Amos Komenský (Comenius), yang menjelaskan kata fauni et satyri sebagai \"dewa hutan dan manusia hutan (si berbulu) adalah hal-hal rekaan\".",
        "en": "They are already mentioned by Jan Amos Komenský (Comenius), who glossed fauni et satyri as \"forest gods and forest men (hairy ones) are invented things\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c05",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Hejkal se podobá východoslovanskému lešijovi, ale také antickým faunům a satyrům, a západoevropským dusům.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hejkal mirip leshy Slavia Timur, juga mirip faun dan satir zaman antik serta dusii Eropa Barat.",
        "en": "The hejkal resembles the East Slavic leshy (lešij), as well as the fauns (faunům) and satyrs (satyrům) of antiquity and the Western European dusii (dusům)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c06",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Ženskou obdobou jsou hejkalky, divoženky a lesní panny.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan perempuannya adalah hejkalky, divoženky, dan lesní panny (gadis hutan).",
        "en": "Its female counterparts are the hejkalky, divoženky and lesní panny (forest maidens)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hejkal-c07",
      "source_id": "hejkal-s1",
      "quote": "Hejkal, československá televizní pohádka, 1978",
      "locator": "V kultuře",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hejkal adalah judul dongeng televisi Cekoslowakia tahun 1978.",
        "en": "Hejkal is the title of a 1978 Czechoslovak television fairy tale."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Herättäjä adalah makhluk legenda Finlandia yang membangunkan orang tidur saat bahaya mengancam, misalnya kebakaran atau pohon tumbang.",
    "en": "The herättäjä is a Finnish legendary being that wakes sleepers when danger threatens, such as fire or a falling tree.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "herattaja-c01",
      "herattaja-c04",
      "herattaja-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Herättäjä adalah makhluk legenda yang membangunkan orang ketika bahaya mengancam, misalnya dengan meneriakkan namanya di telinga atau menariknya dengan keras. Sebagian orang mengaku punya herättäjä sendiri yang menjaga tidur mereka, dan makhluk ini konon pernah menyelamatkan orang dari api dan pohon tumbang. Dalam sebuah cerita Finlandia, seorang lelaki tidur dengan tenang sambil menjaga tungku ter karena yakin herättäjä-nya akan membangunkannya bila tungku menyala. Kalevala juga menyebut pakaian yang berjaga selagi pemiliknya tidur.",
      "en": "The herättäjä is a legendary being that wakes a person when danger threatens, for example by shouting their name in their ear or jerking them hard. Some people claim to have their own herättäjä guarding their sleep, and these beings are said to have saved people from fire and falling trees. In a Finnish story a man slept peacefully while watching a tar pit, sure his herättäjä would wake him if it flared up. The Kalevala also mentions a garment that keeps watch while its owner sleeps.",

        "herattaja-c01",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "herattaja-c01",
      "source_id": "herattaja-s1",
      "quote": "Herättäjä on taruolento, joka herättää ihmisen vaaran uhatessa esimerkiksi huutamalla tämän nimeä tämän korvaan tai jopa tempaisemalla tätä voimakkaasti.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Herättäjä (Pembangun) adalah makhluk legenda yang membangunkan orang saat bahaya mengancam, misalnya dengan meneriakkan namanya di telinga atau bahkan menariknya dengan keras.",
        "en": "The herättäjä (awakener) is a legendary being that wakes a person when danger threatens, for example by shouting their name in their ear or even jerking them hard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herattaja-c02",
      "source_id": "herattaja-s1",
      "quote": "Jotkut väittävät omistavansa oman herättäjän, joka valvoo heidän untaan.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang mengaku punya herättäjä sendiri yang menjaga tidur mereka.",
        "en": "Some people claim to have their own herättäjä watching over their sleep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herattaja-c03",
      "source_id": "herattaja-s1",
      "quote": "Kalevalassa kerrotaan valvovasta vaatteesta: Siinä mies makaelevi, uros unta ottelevi: mies makasi, vaate valvoi.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kalevala menyebut pakaian yang berjaga: \"lelaki itu berbaring, sang jantan tidur: lelaki tidur, pakaian berjaga.\"",
        "en": "The Kalevala tells of a watching garment: \"there the man lies, the male takes his sleep: the man slept, the garment kept watch.\""
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herattaja-c04",
      "source_id": "herattaja-s1",
      "quote": "Herättäjien on kerrottu pelastaneen ihmisiä niin tulelta kuin päälle kaatuvalta puultakin.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Herättäjä konon telah menyelamatkan orang dari api maupun dari pohon yang tumbang menimpa.",
        "en": "Herättäjät are said to have saved people both from fire and from falling trees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herattaja-c05",
      "source_id": "herattaja-s1",
      "quote": "Eräässä suomalaisessa tarinassa henkilö on luottanut herättäjäänsä niin paljon, että hän on tervahaudan kytemistä vahtiessaan voinut rauhassa nukkua.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebuah cerita Finlandia, seseorang begitu memercayai herättäjä-nya sehingga ia bisa tidur tenang sambil menjaga tungku ter yang membara.",
        "en": "In a Finnish story a man trusted his herättäjä so much that he could sleep in peace while watching over a smouldering tar pit."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hippa adalah raja hutan dan roh penguasa hutan dalam tradisi Finlandia, yang dimintai buruan oleh pemburu dan diminta menjauhkan beruang oleh gembala.",
    "en": "Hippa is the king of the forest and forest guardian spirit of Finnish tradition, asked by hunters for game and by herders to keep bears away.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hippa, juga disebut Hiippa, Hiippo, Hippo, Vippo, atau Viipeli Viippo, adalah makhluk legenda Finlandia, raja hutan dan roh penguasa hutan. Ia sering dibayangkan sebagai makhluk lelaki berjanggut, tetapi sesekali juga sebagai perempuan. Namanya mungkin berasal dari penutup kepalanya yang tinggi dan runcing.",
      "en": "Hippa, also called Hiippa, Hiippo, Hippo, Vippo or Viipeli Viippo, is a Finnish legendary being, king of the forest and forest guardian spirit. Hippa was often imagined as a bearded male being, but occasionally as a woman. The name may come from the being's tall, pointed headdress.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hippa-c01",
        "hippa-c03",
        "hippa-c04",
        "hippa-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam banyak mantra pemburu, Hippa dimintai hewan buruan, sedangkan dalam mantra gembala ia diperintahkan menjauhkan beruang dari ternak. Sebuah teks lama menyebutnya raja tertinggi hutan, menyejajarkannya dengan Faunus atau Pan, dan menduga ia sama dengan Kuippana.",
      "en": "In many hunters' charms Hippa is asked for game, while in herders' charms Hippa is ordered to keep bears away from the cattle. An old text calls Hippa the supreme king of the forest, likens Hippa to Faunus or Pan, and suggests Hippa may be the same as Kuippana.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hippa-c02",
        "hippa-c06"
      ]
    }

    "id": "Illhveli (\"paus jahat\") adalah monster laut mitos berwujud paus dalam cerita rakyat Islandia yang menghancurkan kapal dan namanya dianggap tabu.",
    "en": "The illhveli (\"evil whales\") are mythical whale-like sea monsters of Icelandic folklore that smash ships and whose names were taboo.",

    {
      "id": "Illhveli, yang berarti \"paus jahat\" dalam bahasa Islandia, adalah monster laut mitos berwujud paus atau beragam wujud yang konon menghuni perairan Islandia dan agresif terhadap manusia. Orang Islandia membedakan paus \"baik\", yang menolong orang tenggelam, dari paus \"jahat\", yang menghancurkan kapal dan memangsa orang tenggelam; paus sperma, narwhal, dan sebagian paus mitos dianggap jahat. Nama-nama illhveli tabu diucapkan, dan pelanggarnya dihukum tidak diberi makan.",
      "en": "The illhveli, meaning \"evil whales\" in Icelandic, are mythical whale-like or polymorphous sea monsters said to live in Icelandic waters and to be aggressive towards people. Icelanders distinguished \"good\" whales, which helped the drowning, from \"evil\" ones, which smashed ships and devoured the drowning; the sperm whale, the narwhal and some mythical whales were counted as evil. The names of the illhveli were taboo, and anyone who spoke them was left without food.",

      "en": "Why are the names of dangerous creatures often taboo to speak?"
    }
  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "illhveli-s1",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illhveli",
      "title": "Illhveli",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "illhveli-c01",
      "source_id": "illhveli-s1",
      "quote": "Илхвели (исландск. Illhveli — злые киты) — мифические китоподобные или полиморфные морские чудовища, будто бы обитающие в исландских водах. Агрессивны по отношению к людям.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Illhveli (bahasa Islandia: \"paus jahat\") adalah monster laut mitos berwujud paus atau beragam wujud yang konon menghuni perairan Islandia dan bersikap agresif terhadap manusia.",
        "en": "The illhveli (Icelandic \"evil whales\") are mythical whale-like or polymorphous sea monsters said to inhabit Icelandic waters and aggressive towards people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "illhveli-c02",
      "source_id": "illhveli-s1",
      "quote": "У исландцев было наложено табу на их названия, за их произнесение оставляли без пищи.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Islandia memberlakukan tabu atas nama-nama mereka; siapa yang mengucapkannya dihukum tidak diberi makan.",
        "en": "The Icelanders placed a taboo on their names; anyone who spoke them was left without food."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "illhveli-c03",
      "source_id": "illhveli-s1",
      "quote": "По мнению исландцев, существовали «злые» и «добрые» киты. Добрые киты спасают тонущих людей, терпящих крушение, а злые наоборот, сокрушают суда и пожирают утопающих.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut orang Islandia, ada paus \"jahat\" dan paus \"baik\": paus baik menyelamatkan orang yang tenggelam karena kapal karam, sedangkan paus jahat menghancurkan kapal dan memangsa orang yang tenggelam.",
        "en": "According to the Icelanders there were \"evil\" and \"good\" whales: good whales save drowning shipwrecked people, while evil ones smash ships and devour the drowning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "illhveli-c04",
      "source_id": "illhveli-s1",
      "quote": "Злыми считались кашалот и нарвал, а также некоторые мифические киты.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paus sperma dan narwhal, serta sebagian paus mitos, dianggap jahat.",
        "en": "The sperm whale and the narwhal, as well as some mythical whales, were considered evil."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Irudica atau Rudica adalah roh jahat di dalam angin puyuh dalam demonologi rakyat Kroasia, berasal dari sosok Salome dalam Injil yang dikejar petir Tuhan.",
    "en": "Irudica or Rudica is an evil whirlwind spirit of Croatian folk demonology, derived from the Gospel figure of Salome and pursued by God's lightning.",
    "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Irudica, juga Rudica, dalam demonologi rakyat Slavia Barat adalah roh jahat yang berada di dalam angin puyuh. Gagasan ini bertumpu pada transformasi sosok Herodias dan Salome yang digambarkan negatif dalam Injil. Tarian dikecam gereja dan dalam citraan rakyat disamakan dengan angin puyuh, kemudian dengan pesta pernikahan setan.",
      "en": "Irudica, also Rudica, is in West Slavic folk demonology an evil spirit dwelling in the whirlwind. The notion rests on a transformation of the Gospel figures of Herodias and Salome, portrayed negatively. Dances were condemned by the church and in folk imagery identified with the whirlwind, later with a demonic wedding.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "irudica-c01",
        "irudica-c02",

    {
      "id": "Pada orang Kroasia, cucu Herodes Agung dikenal sebagai Poganica dan putrinya Salome sebagai Irudica. Di Dalmatia, Yohanes Pembaptis melindungi orang dari sambaran guntur yang diarahkan Tuhan kepada Irudica, yang dikejar petir dan ditakdirkan mengembara tanpa tempat berlindung. Menurut pengumpul sebuah lagu rakyat Hvar, nama Rudica berasal dari rambutnya yang merah.",
      "en": "Among the Croats the granddaughter of Herod the Great is known as Poganica and her daughter Salome as Irudica. In Dalmatia, John the Baptist protected people from the thunderbolts God aimed at Irudica, who, pursued by lightning, is doomed to wander without shelter.       "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "(хорв. Irudica), также редуцированное Рудица (хорв. Rudica) — в западнославянской народной демонологии злой дух, находящийся в вихре.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Irudica (bahasa Kroasia), juga dalam bentuk pendek Rudica, dalam demonologi rakyat Slavia Barat adalah roh jahat yang berada di dalam angin puyuh.",
        "en": "Irudica (Croatian), also shortened to Rudica, is in West Slavic folk demonology an evil spirit dwelling in the whirlwind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "irudica-c02",
      "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "Метеорологическое представление основано на основе трансформации негативно изображённых в Евангелии образов Иродиады и её дочери Саломеи.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gagasan cuaca ini bertumpu pada transformasi sosok Herodias dan putrinya Salome, yang digambarkan negatif dalam Injil.",
        "en": "This meteorological notion rests on a transformation of the Gospel's negatively portrayed figures of Herodias and her daughter Salome."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "irudica-c03",
      "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "Танцы осуждались церковью и в народной мифо-поэтической образности отождествлялись с вихрем, позже с демонической свадьбой.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tarian dikecam gereja dan dalam citraan mitopoetik rakyat disamakan dengan angin puyuh, kemudian dengan pesta pernikahan setan.",
        "en": "Dances were condemned by the church and in folk mytho-poetic imagery identified with the whirlwind, later with a demonic wedding."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "irudica-c04",
      "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "У хорватов внучка Ирода Великого известна как Poganica (Поганица), а её дочь Саломея как Irudica (Ирудица).",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada orang Kroasia, cucu Herodes Agung dikenal sebagai Poganica, dan putrinya Salome sebagai Irudica.",
        "en": "Among the Croats the granddaughter of Herod the Great is known as Poganica, and her daughter Salome as Irudica."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "irudica-c05",
      "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "В Далмации Иоанн Креститель выступал в качестве защитника от громовых ударов, которые по воле Божьей были направлены против Ирудицы. Спасаясь от молний она обречена скитаться по свету не находя нигде укрытия и изрешечена молниями.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Dalmatia, Yohanes Pembaptis berperan sebagai pelindung dari sambaran guntur yang atas kehendak Tuhan diarahkan kepada Irudica; dikejar petir, ia ditakdirkan mengembara di dunia tanpa menemukan tempat berlindung, tubuhnya berlubang-lubang oleh petir.",
        "en": "In Dalmatia John the Baptist acted as protector against the thunderbolts that God directed at Irudica; fleeing the lightning, she is doomed to wander the world finding no shelter, riddled by lightning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "irudica-c06",
      "source_id": "irudica-s1",
      "quote": "В комментарии к этим стихам собиратель объяснил, что имя Рудица восходит к её рыжим волосам",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam komentar atas lagu rakyat dari Pulau Hvar, pengumpulnya menjelaskan bahwa nama Rudica berasal dari rambutnya yang merah.",
        "en": "In a commentary on a folk song from the island of Hvar, its collector explained that the name Rudica derives from her red hair."
      }
    }

    "en": "The jaroszek is a field demon from Silesia that lures people in the form of a bird or hare but becomes a household helper if caught.",

Orang yang mencoba menangkapnya bisa tenggelam di lumpur, tetapi bila berhasil, jaroszek berubah menjadi demon rumah yang membantu urusan rumah tangga.",
      "en": "The jaroszek is a field demon known in Silesia. It appears in muddy fields, deluding people in the form of a partridge, pheasant or hare. Those who try to catch it may drown in the mud, but if they succeed the jaroszek turns into a household demon that helps with domestic chores.",

      "quote": "Jaroszek pokazuje się na błotnistych polach mamiąc ludzi pod postacią kuropatwy, bażanta lub zająca.",

      "id": "jaroszek-c03",
      "source_id": "jaroszek-s1",
      "quote": "Próba złapania jaroszka może się skończyć utonięciem w błocie, ale jeśli uda się go złapać, wtenczas przeobraża się on w demona domowego, który staje się pomocnikiem w sprawach domowych.",

        "id": "Mencoba menangkap jaroszek bisa berakhir dengan tenggelam di lumpur, tetapi jika berhasil ditangkap, ia berubah menjadi demon rumah yang membantu urusan rumah tangga.",
        "en": "Trying to catch a jaroszek can end in drowning in the mud, but if it is caught it turns into a household demon that helps with domestic matters."

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Tuva, Khaan-Khereti, \"Ratu Burung\", yang dalam bahasa Bashkir disebut Samrigush, adalah burung raksasa yang dapat membawa manusia ke dunia atas atau ke sarangnya. Ia digambarkan sebagai elang atau elang alap-alap raksasa, kadang berapi, dan bisa berbicara dengan bahasa manusia. Ia lebih sering menolong para pahlawan daripada membawa kejahatan, dan memiliki sifat mulia dan kekeluargaan.",
      "en": "In Tuvan mythology Khaan-Khereti, the \"Queen of Birds\", called Samrigush in Bashkir, is a giant bird that can carry people to the upper world or off to its nest. It is pictured as a giant eagle or hawk, sometimes fiery, and can speak human language. It more often helps heroes than brings evil, and has noble and family-minded traits.",

      "en": "In Tuvan and Bashkir tales a hero saves the bird's chicks from a many-headed dragon, and in return the bird promises to lift him to the Upper World.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c01",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "Хаан-Херети (рус. Царица птиц, баш. Самригуш) — в тувинской мифологии это гигантская птица, царица птиц, способная переносить людей в верхний мир или уносить в свое гнездо.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Tuva, Khaan-Khereti (\"Ratu Burung\"; bahasa Bashkir: Samrigush) adalah burung raksasa, ratu para burung, yang dapat membawa manusia ke dunia atas atau ke sarangnya.",
        "en": "In Tuvan mythology Khaan-Khereti (\"Queen of Birds\"; Bashkir Samrigush) is a giant bird, queen of the birds, able to carry people to the upper world or off to its nest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c02",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "Своего рода прототип индийской Гаруды и европейского Феникса. Но в отличие от индийского собрата Хаан-Херети не имеет человеческого лица и не сжигает себя как Феникс.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia semacam prototipe Garuda India dan Feniks Eropa, tetapi berbeda dari kerabat Indianya ia tidak berwajah manusia, dan tidak membakar diri seperti Feniks.",
        "en": "It is a kind of prototype of the Indian Garuda and the European Phoenix, but unlike its Indian relative it has no human face, and it does not burn itself like the Phoenix."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c03",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "Чаще всего она является помощником богатырям, нежели становится носителем зла. Нередко способна разговаривать и наделена некоторыми человеческими чертами как благородство, семейность и т. д.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lebih sering menjadi penolong para pahlawan daripada pembawa kejahatan, sering dapat berbicara, dan memiliki beberapa sifat manusia seperti kemuliaan dan rasa kekeluargaan.",
        "en": "It is more often a helper of heroes than a bearer of evil, can often speak, and has some human traits such as nobility and devotion to family."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c04",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "Гигантская птица в виде орла, чаще ястреба. В некоторых случаях изображается огненной. Умеет говорить на человеческом языке.",
      "locator": "Описание",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia burung raksasa berwujud elang, lebih sering elang alap-alap; kadang digambarkan berapi, dan bisa berbicara dengan bahasa manusia.",
        "en": "It is a giant bird in the form of an eagle, more often a hawk; sometimes depicted as fiery, and able to speak human language."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c05",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "Пример: Батыр спасает птенцов Хаан-Херети (тувинск.) Самригуш (башк.) от прожорливой девятиглавой аждахи (башк.) / пятнадцатиголовый змей (тувинск.), за что птица обещает батыра поднять в Верхний мир.",
      "locator": "Хаан-Херети в литературе",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Contoh motif: seorang batyr (pahlawan) menyelamatkan anak-anak burung Khaan-Khereti (Tuva) atau Samrigush (Bashkir) dari azhdaha berkepala sembilan (Bashkir) atau ular berkepala lima belas (Tuva), dan sebagai balasannya burung itu berjanji mengangkatnya ke Dunia Atas.",
        "en": "An example motif: a batyr (hero) saves the chicks of Khaan-Khereti (Tuvan) or Samrigush (Bashkir) from a voracious nine-headed azhdaha (Bashkir) or fifteen-headed serpent (Tuvan), and in return the bird promises to lift him to the Upper World."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khaan-khereti-c06",
      "source_id": "khaan-khereti-s1",
      "quote": "По тибетской традиции, некоторые тувинцы склонны изображать Хаан-Херети в виде Гаруды и наносить её изображение на флажки.",
      "locator": "Хаан-Херети в литературе",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mengikuti tradisi Tibet, sebagian orang Tuva cenderung menggambarkan Khaan-Khereti dalam wujud Garuda dan melukiskannya pada bendera-bendera kecil.",
        "en": "Following Tibetan tradition, some Tuvans tend to depict Khaan-Khereti as Garuda and put her image on small flags."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kiira atau piharikko adalah makhluk jahat dalam tradisi rakyat Finlandia yang tinggal di pekarangan rumah dan diusir dengan asap ter pada Kamis Putih.",
    "en": "Kiira or piharikko is an evil being of Finnish folk tradition that lives in the farmyard and is driven out with tar smoke on Maundy Thursday.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kiira-c01",
      "kiira-c02",
      "kiira-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi rakyat Finlandia, kiira atau piharikko adalah makhluk jahat yang dikenal di banyak daerah Finlandia Timur dan Barat. Ia kadang datang tinggal di pekarangan rumah dan diusir pada Kamis Pekan Sengsara, Kiirastorstai.",
      "en": "In Finnish folk tradition kiira or piharikko is an evil being known in many parts of eastern and western Finland. It sometimes came to live in the farmyard and was driven out on the Thursday of Holy Week, Kiirastorstai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kiira-c01",
        "kiira-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Kustaa Vilkuna, orang mengusir kiira dengan berlari atau berjalan mengelilingi rumah atau kandang sambil membawa bahan pengusir seperti asap ter, dan mengucapkan mantra seperti \"Kits kiira metsään!\". Penjelasan yang paling umum dari orang tua dahulu adalah agar ular tidak masuk pekarangan pada musim panas.",
      "en": "According to Kustaa Vilkuna, people drove kiira out by running or walking around the house or cowshed with repelling materials such as tar smoke, chanting charms such as \"Kits kiira metsään!\". The old people's most common explanation was that snakes would then stay out of the yard in summer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kiira-c03",
        "kiira-c04",
        "kiira-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vilkuna menilai kiira tetap makhluk yang samar bagi rakyat, dan di balik adat ini tampaknya ada ritus penyucian lama dari zaman Katolik. Kata kiira tampaknya dipinjam dari bahasa Swedia Kuno; padanannya dalam bahasa Swedia modern, \"skär\", berarti murni.",
      "en": "Vilkuna considered kiira a vague being for the people, behind whose custom apparently lies an old purification rite of Catholic times. The word kiira seems to be borrowed from Old Swedish; its modern Swedish counterpart, \"skär\", means pure.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kiira-c06",
        "kiira-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Menurut Vilkuna, pengusiran kiira pada Kamis Putih tampaknya berakar pada ritus penyucian zaman Katolik, sedangkan orang tua dahulu menjelaskannya sebagai cara mencegah ular masuk pekarangan.",
    "en": "According to Vilkuna, driving out kiira on Maundy Thursday seems rooted in a purification rite from Catholic times, while the old people explained it as a way to keep snakes out of the yard.",

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [
    {
      "id": "Bagaimana adat keagamaan bisa berubah menjadi kepercayaan tentang makhluk jahat?",
      "en": "How can a religious custom turn into a belief about an evil being?"
    }
  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kiira-s1",
      "url": "https://fi.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiira_(taruolento)",
      "title": "Kiira (taruolento)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fi)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fi",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kiira-c01",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Kiira eli piharikko on useilla Itä- ja Länsi-Suomen alueilla suomalaisessa kansanperinteessä tunnettu paha, pihapiirissä vaikuttanut uskomusolento.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kiira atau piharikko adalah makhluk kepercayaan jahat dalam tradisi rakyat Finlandia, dikenal di banyak daerah Finlandia Timur dan Barat, yang beraksi di lingkungan pekarangan rumah.",
        "en": "Kiira or piharikko is an evil being of belief in Finnish folk tradition, known in many areas of eastern and western Finland, active around the farmyard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c02",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Kiira tuli toisinaan asumaan talon pihapiiriin, ja tämä paha karkotettiin piinaviikon torstaina eli kiirastorstaina.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kiira kadang datang tinggal di pekarangan rumah, dan makhluk jahat ini diusir pada Kamis Pekan Sengsara, yaitu Kiirastorstai (Kamis Putih).",
        "en": "Kiira sometimes came to live in the farmyard, and this evil was driven out on the Thursday of Holy Week, Kiirastorstai (Maundy Thursday)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c03",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Kustaa Vilkuna toteaa teoksessa Vuotuinen ajantieto ... kiiraa ajettiin tai vedettiin erilaisilla sanonnoilla ja liikkumalla juosten tai rauhallisemmin talon tai navetan ympäri sekä kuljettaen mukana kaikkia mahdollisia karkottavia aineksia, kuten väkeväntuoksuista tervansavua",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Kustaa Vilkuna, kiira diusir atau \"ditarik\" dengan berbagai ucapan sambil berlari atau berjalan mengelilingi rumah atau kandang, membawa segala macam bahan pengusir seperti asap ter yang berbau tajam.",
        "en": "According to Kustaa Vilkuna, kiira was chased or \"dragged\" away with various sayings while running or walking around the house or cowshed, carrying all kinds of repelling things such as pungent tar smoke."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c04",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Taloa kierrettäessä palavan terva-astian ja muun vedettävän kuorman kanssa on saatettu hokea esimerkiksi loitsua Kits kiira metsään!",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sambil mengelilingi rumah dengan wadah ter yang menyala, orang bisa mengucapkan mantra seperti \"Kits kiira metsään!\" (Pergi kiira ke hutan!).",
        "en": "While circling the house with a burning tar pot, people might chant a charm such as \"Kits kiira metsään!\" (Off to the forest, kiira!)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c05",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Vilkunan mukaan vanhan kansan tavallisin selitys kiiran ajamiselle on, että näin menetellen käärmet eivät tulleet kesällä pihaan.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Vilkuna, penjelasan paling umum dari orang tua dahulu untuk pengusiran kiira adalah agar ular tidak datang ke pekarangan pada musim panas.",
        "en": "According to Vilkuna, the old people's most common explanation for driving out kiira was that snakes would then not come into the yard in summer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c06",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Kiira on Vilkunan mukaan jäänyt kansalle epäselväksi olennoksi. Tavan takana on hänen mukaansa ilmeisesti vanha katolisaikainen puhdistusmeno.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Vilkuna, kiira tetap menjadi makhluk yang samar bagi rakyat, dan di balik adat ini tampaknya ada ritus penyucian lama dari zaman Katolik.",
        "en": "According to Vilkuna, kiira remained a vague being to the people, and behind the custom there apparently lies an old purification rite from Catholic times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kiira-c07",
      "source_id": "kiira-s1",
      "quote": "Suomen kielen nimitys kiira on ilmeisesti laina muinaisruotsista. Nykyruotsin vastaava sana on skär 'puhdas'.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Finlandia kiira tampaknya pinjaman dari bahasa Swedia Kuno; padanannya dalam bahasa Swedia modern adalah \"skär\" (murni, bersih).",
        "en": "The Finnish word kiira is apparently a loan from Old Swedish; the corresponding modern Swedish word is \"skär\" (pure)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "claims",
      "searched": "Item Wikidata Q11871411 hanya punya artikel Wikipedia fi; buku Vilkuna (Vuotuinen ajantieto) belum dibuka langsung."
    },
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia fi; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kodinhaltija adalah roh penjaga rumah dalam tradisi rakyat yang tinggal dekat perapian dan diberi bagian makanan agar tidak mengganggu.",
    "en": "The kodinhaltija is a house guardian spirit of folk tradition that lives by the hearth and is given a share of food so it will not cause trouble.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kodinhaltija-c01",
      "kodinhaltija-c03",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi rakyat, kodinhaltija atau kotitonttu adalah haltija atau tonttu yang dipercaya tinggal di rumah hunian. Ia sering dipercaya datang saat api pertama kali dinyalakan di rumah; kadang yang menjadi kodinhaltija adalah orang pertama yang menyalakan api, biasanya pendiri rumah, atau orang pertama yang meninggal di sana. Ia biasanya berdiam di dekat perapian dan kadang membanting pintu tungku sebagai tanda kedatangan tamu.",
      "en": "In folk tradition the kodinhaltija or kotitonttu is a haltija or tonttu believed to live in the house. It was often thought to arrive when a fire was first lit there; sometimes the first fire-maker, often the founder, or the first person to die in the house became the kodinhaltija. It usually stays by the hearth and sometimes bangs the stove door to announce a visitor.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kodinhaltija-c01",
        "kodinhaltija-c02",
        "kodinhaltija-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di meja makan ia sering diberi bagiannya lebih dulu, karena kodinhaltija yang diperlakukan buruk akan mengganggu, dan penampakannya dianggap pertanda kecelakaan atau kematian. Saat rumah baru dibangun, batu perapian, abu, atau tanah dari rumah lama dibawa agar ia ikut pindah.",
      "en": "At meals it was often served first, since a badly treated kodinhaltija would cause trouble, and its appearance was taken as an omen of misfortune or death. When a new house was built, stones, ash or soil from the old hearth were brought along so that it would move too.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kodinhaltija-c04",
        "kodinhaltija-c05"
      ]
    }
